Goal: lr T (enatrec (erase n) (erase b) (erase s)).

We need a lemma: 
**Lemma lr_natrec**: ∀T n b s, lr Nat n → lr T b → lr (Nat→T→T) s → lr T (enatrec n b s).

Proof by well-founded induction on... as discussed: induction on the "numeral structure" of n plus sn of b and s. With the erased relation, let's redo the analysis.

Note lr Nat n = SN_e n. lr T b = ... (SN + closure). lr (Nat→T→T) s = SN_e s ∧ ∀ v, SN_e v → lr (T→T) (eapp s v).

We want lr T (enatrec n b s). Let's think about using the candidate properties: cand (lr ρ T) gives CR3': if enatrec n b s is not an evalue and all its one-step reducts are lr T, then lr T (enatrec n b s) ✓. Is enatrec ever an evalue? No ✓. So it suffices to show all one-step reducts of enatrec n b s are lr T, and (by CR3' we get lr T). But CR3' requires all reducts related; the reducts are:
1. enatrec n' b s with n --> n' (EST_RecArg): need lr T (enatrec n' b s). We have lr Nat n' (CR2 of SN) ✓, same b, s → apply the lemma recursively at n' — induction on SN_e n ✓.
2. enatrec n b' s with n numeric_value_e, b --> b' (EST_RecBase): need lr T (enatrec n b' s). We have lr T b' (CR2) ✓ and n numeric. Recursion on SN_e b ✓.
3. enatrec n b s' with n numeric, b evalue, s --> s' (EST_RecStep): need lr T (enatrec n b s'). Recursion on SN_e s ✓.
4. enatrec zero b s --> b with b, s evalues (EST_RecZero): lr T b ✓.
5. enatrec (succ n) b s --> eapp (eapp s n) (enatrec n b s) with n numeric_value_e, b, s evalues: need lr T (eapp (eapp s n) (enatrec n b s)).
   - lr Nat n ✓ (numeric_value_e → SN_e; prove).
   - lr (T→T) (eapp s n) from lr (Nat→T→T) s applied to n ✓.
   - Then lr T (eapp (eapp s n) (enatrec n b s)) requires lr T (enatrec n b s) — where n is the predecessor numeral. Apply the lemma recursively at n (same b, s). Recursion on what? n is a subterm of (succ n) syntactically... but SN_e (succ n) derivation: if n is an evalue, succ n is an evalue (no reducts), so SN_e (succ n) is trivial and doesn't give SN_e n directly. But numeric_value_e n → SN_e n (provable separately). Hmm, the induction measure needs to handle this case.

So the measure: we need something that decreases at case 5. Options: induction on the *numeral* n (structural), combined with SN_e of b and s as discussed. OK, standard technique for this: use the **SN derivation of the whole term** or a **"numeral-or-reduct" well-founded order**: define a relation on n: n' ⊏ n iff n' is a reduct of n, OR n = succ n' (and n numeric). Is this well-founded? For terminating terms (SN), the reflexive-transitive closure of "reduct" combined with "predecessor of a numeral" — is it well-founded? A numeral n = succ n0: n0 ⊏ n. Alternative: define the relation "n is numerically equivalent to a numeral": For the recursion case, the key is that the recursion on numerals terminates (external nat) and the recursion on b/s reductions uses SN. Let's structure the proof as follows:

Main lemma: ∀T b s n, lr Nat n → lr T b → lr (Nat→T→T) s → lr T (enatrec n b s).

Proof by **well-founded induction on the pair (SN_e b, SN_e s)** *nested inside* an induction on `n`'s structure? Hmm, case 1 changes n not b/s.

What if we prove it by induction on **SN_e n** and handle the numeral case by a separate induction on the numeral k, where within that induction we do a nested induction on (SN_e b, SN_e s)? The tricky part is when n is a numeral: case 5 recurses to a smaller numeral ✓ (induction on k), and cases 2,3 recurse on b, s (nested induction). And case 1 doesn't occur for numerals ✓. So:

Lemma A: ∀n b s T, SN_e n → lr T b → lr (Nat→T→T) s → lr T (enatrec n b s).
Proof by induction on SN_e n (with b, s, T generalized). Given n:
- For any reduct n' of n: IH gives lr T (enatrec n' b s) ✓ (case 1).
- If n is a numeral (numeric_value_e): need a separate argument. Note SN_e n's derivation is trivial, so the IH gives nothing useful. So we need Lemma B for numerals:
  Lemma B: ∀k b s T, lr T b → lr (Nat→T→T) s → lr T (enatrec (numeral_e k) b s).
  Proof by induction on k, with a nested induction on... the b/s reductions. Hmm, but Lemma B's proof for k = S k' needs the IH at k' with the *same* b and s ✓ (induction on k). But cases 2,3 (b/s reductions) need recursion at the same k with b' or s'. So within the k-induction, we need induction on SN_e b and SN_e s. But those inductions' recursive calls at case 5 go back to k' < k (fine — the k-IH is available regardless of b, s), and at cases 2,3 stay at the same k with smaller b/s.
  
  So Lemma B: prove by induction on k: P(k) := ∀b s T, lr T b → lr (Nat→T→T) s → lr T (enatrec (numeral_e k) b s).
  To prove P(k), we need to handle b/s reductions, which requires an inner induction on SN_e b and SN_e s. Since P(k) is universally quantified over b and s, we can structure: for fixed k, prove Q_k(b) := ∀s T, lr T b → lr (Nat→T→T) s → lr T (enatrec (numeral_e k) b s) by induction on SN_e b? But Q_k(b) must be proven for all b with lr T b (which implies SN_e b), so we can do `induction on SN_e b` — but the statement must be general enough: when b --> b', we need Q_k(b'). Since we're doing induction on the SN_e derivation of the specific b, and lr T b' follows from CR2, the IH at b' applies ✓.
  
  Within Q_k(b) for fixed b, we need induction on SN_e s for case 3. So nest another induction: R_{k,b}(s) := ∀T, lr T b → lr (Nat→T→T) s → lr T (enatrec (numeral_e k) b s), proven by induction on SN_e s. Case 3 (s --> s') uses the s-IH ✓. Case 5 (k = S k', n = succ (numeral k')) needs P(k') at b and s — available from the outer k-induction ✓ (P(k') is for all b, s). Case 2 (b --> b') uses the b-IH ✓ — but careful: inside the s-induction, when b --> b' we need to call Q_k(b'), which is available from the b-induction's IH (the b-induction was done first, with s and T generalized ✓). Let me restructure more cleanly. Define:

Lemma B_gen: ∀k b s T, lr T b → lr (Nat→T→T) s → lr T (enatrec (numeral_e k) b s).

Proof by induction on k.
  k = 0:
    Need ∀b s T, lr T b → lr (Nat→T→T) s → lr T (enatrec ezero b s).

        - EST_RecStep: numeric_value_e ezero ✓ and evalue b and estep s s' → reduct enatrec ezero b s' → IH_s at s' ✓ (needs lr T b ✓ and lr (Nat→T→T) s' ✓).

        Use CR3' at T: reducts:

To do induction on SN_e b, we can use `remember (erase? )`... Actually, we can just do `induction Hb_sn` where Hb_sn : strongly_normalizing_e b derived from lr T b. But lr T b is not of the form strongly_normalizing_e b, so we need to generalize: e.g.,

  assert (H : forall b, strongly_normalizing_e b -> forall s T, lr T b -> lr (Nat→T→T) s -> lr T (enatrec (numeral_e k) b s)).

In Rocq: `induction 1` on a hypothesis of type strongly_normalizing_e b won't generalize s, T automatically. We should write the lemma with the SN hypothesis explicit and do `intros b Hsn; induction Hsn as [b Hstep IH]; intros s T Hb Hs.` — but the IH would be about the specific b' from Hstep and the statement would be `for all s T, lr T b' -> ...` if we set it up right. Lemma B0 : forall b, SN_e b -> forall s, SN_e s -> forall T, lr T b -> lr (Nat→T→T) s -> lr T (enatrec ezero b s).
Proof. intros b HSNb. induction HSNb as [b Hstepb IHb]. intros s HSNs. induction HSNs as [s Hsteps IHs]. intros T Hb Hs. ...
- EST_RecBase case: reduct enatrec ezero b' s → apply IHb b' Hstepb ... : IHb : forall b', estep b b' -> forall s, SN_e s -> forall T, lr T b' -> ... So IHb b' Hb'step s HSNs T Hb' Hs ✓.
- EST_RecStep case: reduct enatrec ezero b s' → apply IHs s' Hstep T Hb Hs' ✓.
✓ This works because IHb and IHs are generalized appropriately.

Now, the T_Abs case in detail. We're proving the fundamental theorem:

**Fundamental theorem (FT)**: ∀Δ Γ t T, has_type Δ Γ t T → ∀ρA ρB σ, cand_val ρA ρB → (∀x A, lookup_context x Γ = Some A → lr ρA ρB A (σ x)) → lr ρA ρB T (subst_e σ (erase t)).

where cand_val ρA ρB := (∀X, cand (ρA X)) ∧ (∀i, cand (ρB i)).

Proof by induction on the typing derivation. Cases:

T = Ty_All A: P u = SN_e u ∧ ∀C, cand C → lr ρA (cons C ρB) A (etapp u).

  - CR2: given P u, estep u u'. SN ✓. Second: ∀C cand, lr (cons C ρB) A (etapp u'); from lr (cons C ρB) A (etapp u) and estep (etapp u) (etapp u') by EST_TApp, CR2 of lr A (cand by IH at A with cand_val extended ✓) ✓.

So ✓.
✓ All good, and the induction is structural on the type ✓ (the All case uses the body A with the extended valuation; IH at A requires cand_val for the extended valuation ✓).

Now, the relation definition and the correspondence lemma. Let me write out the formal definitions:

Definition tymapA := atom -> etm -> Prop.
Definition tymapB := nat -> etm -> Prop.

Fixpoint lr (ρA : tymapA) (ρB : tymapB) (T : ty) : etm -> Prop :=
  match T with
  | Ty_BVar i => ρB i
  | Ty_FVar X => ρA X
  | Ty_Arrow T1 T2 => fun u => sn_e u /\ forall v, lr ρA ρB T1 v -> lr ρA ρB T2 (eapp u v)
  | Ty_All T1 => fun u => sn_e u /\ forall (C : etm -> Prop), cand C -> lr ρA (consB C ρB) T1 (etapp u)
  | Ty_Nat => fun u => sn_e u
  end.

Hmm, careful: `lr ρA (consB C ρB) T1` — the `consB` operation: fun i => match i with 0 => C | S j => ρB j end.

Wait, there's an issue with the All case and the de Bruijn indices: the body T1's indices are relative to T1, where index 0 is the All-bound variable and indices j+1 correspond to outer binders. But the *enclosing* lr call's ρB maps outer binders... For a type T1 that is the body of Ty_All, when we interpret the whole type Ty_All T1 at the top level of an lr call with valuation ρB, the body's outer references (indices ≥ 1 in T1) correspond to the outer context of the Ty_All type, which is ρB. So consB C ρB maps T1's index 0 ↦ C ✓ and T1's index (S j) ↦ ρB j ✓. Good.

But hmm, careful: the whole type Ty_All T1 at the top level has its own free indices? A type at the top level with free de Bruijn indices would be dangling; wf types have none. But intermediate calls: lr is called on the body? No — in the All case we call `lr ρA (consB C ρB) T1` on the body T1 ✓, and T1's indices are interpreted consistently ✓. In the arrow case we call lr on subterms which are in the same context ✓.

Now, the correspondence lemma: lr ρA ρB (open_ty_rec k U T) u ↔ lr ρA (ρB[k ↦ lr ρA ρB U]) T u — hmm, but here's the shift issue again: `open_ty_rec k U T` replaces index k; the remaining indices < k are the same; on the RHS, T's indices < k are interpreted as ρB i ✓ (same) ✓. So the statement should be fine with the *same* ρB on both sides, provided T's free indices are within 0..k. Let me re-derive: T's free indices ≤ k (lc_ty_at (S k) T means free indices < S k, so ≤ k ✓). LHS interprets the opened type: its free indices are T's free indices except k. For i < k: the opened type has index i (unchanged) interpreted as ρB i ✓; RHS: T's index i interpreted by (ρB[k↦C_U]) i = ρB i ✓ (i ≠ k). ✓ For U's indices: U is locally closed, no free indices ✓. But wait: what if T has free indices *inside* U's substitution? No, U contributes only its own structure ✓.

But there's another subtlety: the constraint on T should be "T's free indices are < S k", but what about deeper occurrences under binders? lc_ty_at (S k) T handles depth correctly ✓ (bound indices inside T increase the allowed threshold).

Hmm, wait: actually there's still the issue I raised earlier: in the All case of the correspondence, the body B has lc_ty_at (S (S k)) and we apply the IH at k' = S k. The LHS: lr ρA (cons C ρB) (open_ty_rec (S k) U B) vs RHS: lr ρA ((cons C ρB)[S k ↦ lr ρA (cons C ρB) U]) B = lr ρA (cons C (ρB[k ↦ lr ρA (cons C ρB) U])) B. Let me think about the term substitution environment more carefully, because there's a subtlety with the erased step relation and the environment: the FT conclusion is lr T (subst_e σ (erase t)). For T_Abs, we need the substitution lemma for open_e and subst_e, plus the "app_abs_lr" lemma.

Let me write out the exact statements:

**subst_open_e**: ∀(b : etm) (x : atom) (v : etm) (σ : atom → etm) (σ' : atom → etm),
   (σ' x = v) → (∀y, y ≠ x → σ' y = σ y) → (~ In x (fv_e b)) → 
   subst_e σ' (open_e b (efvar x)) = open_e (subst_e σ b) v.

Hmm, do we need ~In x (fv_e b)? Let's re-derive: LHS = subst_e σ' (open_e b (efvar x)): opening replaces bvar 0 with efvar x; then substituting: free vars of b get σ'; the introduced efvar x gets σ' x = v. RHS = open_e (subst_e σ b) v: free vars of b get σ; then bvar 0 replaced by v. These agree if free x's in b get the same treatment: LHS gives σ' x = v; RHS gives σ x. So we need x ∉ FV(b) OR σ x = v. In our use, σ' = [x ↦ v]σ so σ' x = v and σ x arbitrary; hence need x ∉ FV(b) ✓. Alternatively, choose σ' x = σ x? No, we need σ' x = v. So freshness needed ✓.

Hmm, but wait: in the T_Abs case, do we actually need x ∉ FV(body)? The typing premise is ∀x∉L, has_type ... (open_tm body (fvar x)) T2. We can choose x ∉ L ∪ FV(body) ✓ (FV(body) finite). ✓.

Also, does erase(body) have the same free variables as body? Yes ✓ (erasure preserves free term variables).

**app_abs_lr**: ∀T1 T2 b, (∀v, lr ρA ρB T1 v → lr ρA ρB T2 (open_e b v)) → ∀v, lr ρA ρB T1 v → lr ρA ρB T2 (eapp (eabs b) v).
Proof: given v ∈ lr T1. Prove by induction on SN_e v (which we get from CR1 of lr T1). For the induction, we need the statement generalized? The statement at v: "∀v, lr T1 v → lr T2 (eapp (eabs b) v)". Proof by induction on the SN derivation of v:
  - Given Hsn : SN_e v. Reducts of eapp (eabs b) v:
    * EST_AppAbs requires evalue v → open_e b v ∈ lr T2 by hypothesis ✓.
    * EST_App2 requires evalue (eabs b) ✓ and estep v v' → eapp (eabs b) v' — need lr T2: by the IH at v' (which has SN_e v' from Hsn's step) applied to lr T1 v' (CR2 of lr T1) ✓.
    * EST_App1 impossible (eabs doesn't step) ✓.
    Then CR3' at T2 gives lr T2 ✓ (app not evalue ✓).
  So the induction: `intros v Hsn; induction Hsn as [v Hstep IH]; intros Hv; apply CR3' ...` — need IH : ∀v', estep v v' → ∀?? The induction principle gives IH : ∀v', estep v v' → sn_e v' (the premise) and the property at v' if the statement is `∀v, sn_e v → ...`. Let me state: `∀v, sn_e v → ∀(Hv : lr T1 v), lr T2 (eapp (eabs b) v)`. Hmm, the induction on `sn_e v` with v fixed: `induction 1` on the hypothesis sn_e v. Coq's induction on an inductive *in Prop* with the term v... Let me write: 
  Lemma app_abs_lr_gen : forall v, sn_e v -> forall (Hv : lr T1 v), lr T2 (eapp (eabs b) v). Then `intros v H; induction H as [v Hstep IH].` gives IH : ∀v', estep v v' → sn_e v'? No: the induction principle for sn_e (like Acc): sn_e v := ∀v', estep v v' → sn_e v'. The induction principle (with the motive P(v) := sn_e v → forall Hv, lr ...): Coq's `induction H` where H : sn_e v gives goal P v; Coq generates the constructor case: for v with Hstep : ∀v', estep v v' → sn_e v' and IH : ∀v', estep v v' → P v'... Actually since sn_e's constructor has the recursive argument under a forall, Coq's induction principle is:
  sn_e_ind : ∀P, (∀v, (∀v', estep v v' → sn_e v') → (∀v', estep v v' → P v') → P v) → ∀v, sn_e v → P v.
  So `induction H as [v Hstep IH]` with H : sn_e v gives IH : ∀v', estep v v' → P v'. But P must be stated as a function of v only; since our goal has extra hypotheses, we need to generalize them before induction: e.g., 
  `intros v H. induction H as [v Hstep IH]. intros Hv. ...` — here P(v) := sn_e v → ... no. If the goal is `forall (Hv : lr T1 v), lr T2 (...)`, then P(v) := ∀(Hv : lr T1 v), lr T2 (eapp (eabs b) v) ✓; after `induction H`, the goal becomes P v with IH : ∀v', estep v v' → P v' ✓. But P v is `lr T1 v → lr T2 (...)`: we need `lr T1 v` as an assumption that is NOT part of P; so P(v) = lr T1 v → ... ✓ and the goal after induction is P v; then `intros Hv` ✓; and IH v' Hstep' : P v' = lr T1 v' → lr T2 (...) ✓. Since P doesn't mention sn_e v (the inductive), the induction works ✓.

  Hmm, but P mentions `lr T1 v` where v is the inductive variable — fine ✓.

OK. Now the FT statement. Let me write it:

Definition env_rel (ρA ρB) (σ : atom → etm) (Γ : context) : Prop :=
  ∀x A, lookup_context x Γ = Some A → lr ρA ρB A (σ x).

Theorem fundamental : ∀Δ Γ t T, has_type Δ Γ t T →
  ∀ρA ρB σ, cand_val ρA ρB → env_rel ρA ρB σ Γ → lr ρA ρB T (subst_e σ (erase t)).

Proof by induction on the derivation:
- T_Var: env_rel gives lr T (σ x) ✓; subst_e σ (erase (tm_fvar x)) = subst_e σ (efvar x) = σ x ✓.
- T_Abs: as described.
- T_App: IH1, IH2 with same ρ, σ, env ✓.
- T_TAbs: as described, with the premise at fresh X.
- T_TApp: as described.
- T_Zero, T_Succ ✓.
- T_Rec: IH's give lr Nat (subst_e σ (erase n)), lr T (... b), lr (Nat→T→T) (... s) → apply the natrec lemma ✓. Note: the natrec lemma works on erased terms: lr T (enatrec (erase n') ...) — but subst_e σ (erase (tm_natrec n b s)) = enatrec (subst_e σ (erase n)) (...) ✓ (subst_e commutes with natrec ✓). One more consideration: the FT's T_Abs case requires the IH at the premise with the extended context. The premise is `forall x, ~ In x L -> has_type Delta (update Gamma x T1) (open_tm t2 (tm_fvar x)) T2`. So the IH (the theorem being proven by induction) applied to that subderivation requires specifying x. In Coq, when we do `induction H` on the has_type derivation, the T_Abs case gives us the premise as a hypothesis (a forall statement) and the IH as an assumption: for all x, ~In x L → P(that derivation)... Actually with the cofinite quantification, the induction principle generated by Coq for has_type: the T_Abs constructor has an argument `(forall x, ~ In x L -> has_type ...)`. Coq's induction principle will give, for the T_Abs case: H : ∀x, ~In x L → has_type ..., and IH : ∀x, ~In x L → P(x, ...) — i.e., the induction hypothesis is stated for all x ∉ L ✓. 

But our goal (the FT) is a statement about t, T, and has extra hypotheses (ρ, σ, env). When doing `induction Hty`, we need to generalize over ρ σ env before the induction so the IHs are general enough. Let me state the FT with all parameters as implications and do:

Theorem fundamental : forall Delta Gamma t T, has_type Delta Gamma t T -> forall (ρA : ...) (ρB : ...) (σ : atom -> etm), cand_val ρA ρB -> env_rel ρA ρB σ Gamma -> lr ρA ρB T (subst_e σ (erase t)).
Proof.
  intros Delta Gamma t T Hty.
  induction Hty; intros ρA ρB σ Hcand Henv.
  ...
  
Hmm, but `induction Hty` with the goal being `forall ρA ρB σ, ...` — the IHs will be generalized over ρA ρB σ? Coq's induction on Hty will produce IHs of the form `forall ρA ρB σ, cand_val ... -> env_rel ... -> lr ...` only if we generalize before induction: the standard approach is:
  intros Delta Gamma t T Hty.
  induction Hty; intros ...
Actually, when the goal is `forall ρA ρB σ, ...` and we `induction Hty`, Coq will try to keep the conclusion's local context; the IHs are created from the goal, which at that point is `forall ρA ρB σ, ...` (since we introduced Hty last). Hmm, `intros Delta Gamma t T Hty` leaves the goal `forall ρA ρB σ, cand_val -> env_rel -> lr ...`. Then `induction Hty` — Coq will generalize the goal over the remaining forall-bound variables? By default, `induction Hty` will do `generalize dependent` on variables that depend on the inducted term... The safest is:

  intros Delta Gamma t T Hty.
  induction Hty; intros ρA ρB σ Hcand Henv.
  
and hope the IHs are generalized. Actually, this standard pattern (intros H; induction H; intros ...) yields IHs that are generalized over the remaining variables only if they were in the goal at induction time. The goal at induction time is `forall ρA ρB σ, cand_val ρA ρB -> env_rel ρA ρB σ Gamma -> lr ρA ρB T (subst_e σ (erase t))`. Since ρA ρB σ are bound in the goal, Coq's induction will produce subgoals with the IHs of the form `forall ρA ρB σ, ...` — hmm, I believe when you do `induction H` and the goal is a forall, Coq does NOT automatically generalize; it will complain or produce IHs where the forall is still there? Let me recall: `induction H` replaces the goal by the constructor cases; the induction hypotheses are derived from the *current goal*, which includes the forall. Actually, Coq's `induction` only generalizes the *dependent hypotheses*, and the conclusion's foralls stay in the goal; the IHs are formed from the goal as-is. So the IH for a recursive argument would be `forall ρA ρB σ, cand_val → env_rel → lr ...` if the goal has the foralls. Yes, I believe that's right: induction works on the goal, and the goal at that point is the full forall, so each IH is the full forall statement ✓. This is the standard idiom in many proofs: `intros; induction H; intros; ...`.

Hmm, but the subtlety: `induction Hty` where Hty : has_type Delta Gamma t T and the goal has `t`, `T`, `Gamma`, `Delta` — these are variables in the context, and Coq's `induction` will substitute them per case; the remaining goal (with the foralls) becomes the case goal ✓. The IHs are for the recursive premises, instantiated at their terms ✓ and generalized over the foralls ✓. This is standard ✓.

Let me write the first chunk:

```coq
(* ===== Erased syntax ===== *)

Inductive etm : Type :=
  | etm_bvar : nat -> etm
  | etm_fvar : atom -> etm
  | etm_abs : etm -> etm
  | etm_app : etm -> etm -> etm
  | etm_tabs : etm -> etm
  | etm_tapp : etm -> etm
  | etm_zero : etm
  | etm_succ : etm -> etm
  | etm_natrec : etm -> etm -> etm -> etm.

Fixpoint erase (t : tm) : etm :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar i => etm_bvar i
  | tm_fvar x => etm_fvar x
  | tm_abs T t1 => etm_abs (erase t1)
  | tm_app t1 t2 => etm_app (erase t1) (erase t2)
  | tm_tabs t1 => etm_tabs (erase t1)
  | tm_tapp t1 T => etm_tapp (erase t1)
  | tm_zero => etm_zero
  | tm_succ t1 => etm_succ (erase t1)
  | tm_natrec n b s => etm_natrec (erase n) (erase b) (erase s)
  end.

Fixpoint open_e_rec (k : nat) (u t : etm) : etm :=
  match t with
  | etm_bvar i => if Nat.eqb k i then u else etm_bvar i
  | etm_fvar x => etm_fvar x
  | etm_abs t1 => etm_abs (open_e_rec (S k) u t1)
  | etm_app t1 t2 => etm_app (open_e_rec k u t1) (open_e_rec k u t2)
  | etm_tabs t1 => etm_tabs (open_e_rec (S k) u t1)
  | etm_tapp t1 => etm_tapp (open_e_rec k u t1)
  | etm_zero => etm_zero
  | etm_succ t1 => etm_succ (open_e_rec k u t1)
  | etm_natrec n b s => etm_natrec (open_e_rec k u n) (open_e_rec k u b) (open_e_rec k u s)
  end.

Definition open_e (t u : etm) : etm := open_e_rec 0 u t.

Fixpoint subst_e (σ : atom -> etm) (t : etm) : etm :=
  match t with
  | etm_bvar i => etm_bvar i
  | etm_fvar x => σ x
  | etm_abs t1 => etm_abs (subst_e σ t1)
  | etm_app t1 t2 => etm_app (subst_e σ t1) (subst_e σ t2)
  | etm_tabs t1 => etm_tabs (subst_e σ t1)
  | etm_tapp t1 => etm_tapp (subst_e σ t1)
  | etm_zero => etm_zero
  | etm_succ t1 => etm_succ (subst_e σ t1)
  | etm_natrec n b s => etm_natrec (subst_e σ n) (subst_e σ b) (subst_e σ s)
  end.
```

Hmm, one important check: `tm_tabs` in the annotated syntax: `open_tm_ty t T` substitutes types into annotations, leaving term structure. So erase (open_tm_ty t T) = erase t ✓.

```coq
Inductive estep : etm -> etm -> Prop :=
  | EST_AppAbs : forall b v, evalue v -> estep (etm_app (etm_abs b) v) (open_e b v)
  | EST_App1 : forall t1 t1' t2, estep t1 t1' -> estep (etm_app t1 t2) (etm_app t1' t2)
  | EST_App2 : forall v1 t2 t2', evalue v1 -> estep t2 t2' -> estep (etm_app v1 t2) (etm_app v1 t2')
  | EST_TAppTabs : forall b, estep (etm_tapp (etm_tabs b)) b
  | EST_TApp : forall t t', estep t t' -> estep (etm_tapp t) (etm_tapp t')
  | EST_Succ : forall t t', estep t t' -> estep (etm_succ t) (etm_succ t')

  | EST_RecBase : forall n b b' s, numeric_value_e n -> estep b b' -> estep (etm_natrec n b s) (etm_natrec n b' s)
  | EST_RecStep : forall n b s s', numeric_value_e n -> evalue b -> estep s s' -> estep (etm_natrec n b s) (etm_natrec n b s')
  | EST_RecZero : forall b s, evalue b -> evalue s -> estep (etm_natrec etm_zero b s) b
```

```coq
Definition consB (C : etm -> Prop) (rho : nat -> etm -> Prop) : nat -> etm -> Prop :=
  fun i => match i with 0 => C | S j => rho j end.

Fixpoint lr (rhoA : atom -> etm -> Prop) (rhoB : nat -> etm -> Prop) (T : ty) {struct T} : etm -> Prop :=
  match T with
  | Ty_BVar i => rhoB i
  | Ty_FVar X => rhoA X
  | Ty_Arrow T1 T2 => fun u => sn_e u /\ (forall v, lr rhoA rhoB T1 v -> lr rhoA rhoB T2 (etm_app u v))
```

```coq
Definition consB (C : etm -> Prop) (rho : nat -> etm -> Prop) : nat -> etm -> Prop :=
  fun i => match i with
           | O => C
           | S j => rho j
           end.

Definition updB (rho : nat -> etm -> Prop) (k : nat) (C : etm -> Prop) : nat -> etm -> Prop :=
  fun i => if Nat.eqb i k then C else rho i.

Fixpoint lr (rhoA : atom -> etm -> Prop) (rhoB : nat -> etm -> Prop) (T : ty) : etm -> Prop :=
  match T with
  | Ty_BVar i => rhoB i
  | Ty_FVar X => rhoA X
  | Ty_Nat => sn_e
  | Ty_Arrow T1 T2 =>
      fun u => sn_e u /\ (forall v, lr rhoA rhoB T1 v -> lr rhoA rhoB T2 (etm_app u v))
  | Ty_All T1 =>
      fun u => sn_e u /\ (forall C, cand C -> lr rhoA (consB C rhoB) T1 (etm_tapp u))
  end.
```

Lemma sn_erase : forall u, sn_e u -> forall t, erase t = u -> strongly_normalizing t.
Proof.
  intros u H. induction H as [u Hstep IH]. intros t Heq. apply SN_intro. intros t' Ht'.
  apply IH with (u' := erase t').  (* IH : forall u', estep u u' -> forall t, erase t = u' -> SN t *)
  - rewrite Heq. apply erase_step. assumption.
  - reflexivity.
Qed.
Wait, the IH shape: for `induction H as [u Hstep IH]` where H : sn_e u, the goal is `forall t, erase t = u -> strongly_normalizing t`. P(u) := forall t, erase t = u -> SN t. Then IH : forall u', estep u u' -> P u' = forall t, erase t = u' -> SN t ✓. Then for t' with step t t': need SN t'; apply IH with (u' := erase t'): need estep u (erase t') ✓ from Hstep (erase t') (rewrite Heq; apply erase_step; assumption) and erase t' = erase t' ✓. ✓

```coq
Lemma lr_mapB_agree : forall T m, lc_ty_at m T ->
```

Now the opening correspondence:
```coq
Lemma lr_open_ty : forall T k U rhoA rhoB u,
  lc_ty_at 0 U ->
  (lr rhoA rhoB (open_ty_rec k U T) u <-> lr rhoA (updB rhoB k (lr rhoA rhoB U)) T u).
```

Hmm, but the induction on T with k generalized; the All case needs the IH at k := S k with the valuation (consB C rhoB) and compares updB (consB C rhoB) (S k) (lr rhoA (consB C rhoB) U) with consB C (updB rhoB k (lr rhoA rhoB U)). We need these valuations pointwise equivalent, then lr_mapB_agree (no lc needed?) — hmm, lr_mapB_agree requires lc_ty_at m B for some m; but the requirement was for closed types only. Here we'd need: two valuations that are pointwise equivalent give equivalent lr — that's lr_mapB_equiv (pointwise at every index, no lc) ✓. So prove lr_mapB_equiv:
```coq
Lemma lr_mapB_equiv : forall T rhoA rho rho', (forall i u, rho i u <-> rho' i u) -> forall u, lr rhoA rho T u <-> lr rhoA rho' T u.
```
By induction on T ✓.
And then show the pointwise equivalence of updB (consB C rhoB) (S k) (lr rhoA (consB C rhoB) U) and consB C (updB rhoB k (lr rhoA rhoB U)):
  For index i:
  - i = 0: LHS = (consB C rhoB) 0 = C? Hmm, updB (consB C rhoB) (S k) X 0 = if 0 =? S k then X else (consB C rhoB) 0 = C ✓ (since 0 ≠ S k). RHS = consB C (...) 0 = C ✓.
  - i = S j: LHS = if S j =? S k then X else (consB C rhoB) (S j) = if j =? k then X else rhoB j. RHS = consB C (updB rhoB k Y) (S j) = updB rhoB k Y j = if j =? k then Y else rhoB j. So need X ↔ Y when j = k, where X = lr rhoA (consB C rhoB) U and Y = lr rhoA rhoB U; and since U is locally closed (lc_ty_at 0 U), lr_mapB_agree with m=0 gives X ↔ Y ✓.
  ✓.

So the lr_open_ty proof needs: lr_mapB_equiv, lr_mapB_agree (for the closed U), Nat.eqb facts, and case analysis.

Let me now think about how to structure the proof of lr_open_ty in Coq. The statement:
```coq
Lemma lr_open_ty : forall T, forall k U rhoA rhoB u,
  lc_ty_at 0 U ->
  lr rhoA rhoB (open_ty_rec k U T) u <-> lr rhoA (updB rhoB k (lr rhoA rhoB U)) T u.
Proof.
  induction T; intros k U rhoA rhoB u HlcU; simpl.
```
Hmm, `simpl` on `open_ty_rec k U T` for T = Ty_BVar i gives `if k =? i then U else Ty_BVar i` ✓; on the RHS, `lr rhoA (updB ...) (Ty_BVar i) u` simplifies to `updB ... i u` ✓ — but `updB` is a Definition, and `simpl` might unfold it; `lr` on Ty_BVar gives `rhoB i` ✓. Then the RHS is `(if i =? k then lr rhoA rhoB U else rhoB i) u`. Hmm, the condition order: updB is `if Nat.eqb i k` while open_ty_rec uses `if Nat.eqb k i` — symmetric ✓ (Nat.eqb is symmetric propositionally; but for the case analysis we can use `Nat.eqb_eq`).

Let me write the proof carefully per case, using `destruct (Nat.eqb k i) eqn:E` and `apply Nat.eqb_eq in E` / `apply Nat.eqb_neq in E` ✓.

Now, the FT's T_TApp case instantiates lr_open_ty with k=0: `lr rhoA rhoB (open_ty_rec 0 U T) u ↔ lr rhoA (updB rhoB 0 (lr rhoA rhoB U)) T u`, and `updB rhoB 0 C` = consB C rhoB? Pointwise: updB rhoB 0 C 0 = C ✓; updB rhoB 0 C (S j) = if S j =? 0 then C else rhoB (S j) = rhoB (S j). And consB C rhoB (S j) = rhoB j. Hmm! Mismatch: consB shifts (ρB j) whereas updB keeps (ρB (S j)). 

Ah, here's the shift issue again! Let me recheck. In the All definition: lr rhoA rhoB (Ty_All T1) u = sn_e u ∧ ∀C, cand C → lr rhoA (consB C rhoB) T1 (etapp u). Here consB maps T1's index 0 ↦ C and T1's index (S j) ↦ rhoB j.

In the correspondence lr_open_ty with k=0: lr rhoA rhoB (open_ty_rec 0 U T) u ↔ lr rhoA (updB rhoB 0 (lr rhoA rhoB U)) T u. For T's index (S j): LHS's opened type has index (S j) (unchanged) interpreted as rhoB (S j); RHS's valuation updB rhoB 0 maps (S j) ↦ rhoB (S j) ✓ consistent. But consB C rhoB maps (S j) ↦ rhoB j. So updB rhoB 0 C and consB C rhoB are DIFFERENT valuations (they differ on indices ≥ 1). Which one does the correspondence need? Let me re-derive.

We have has_type Δ Γ t (Ty_All T) and IH gives lr rhoA rhoB (Ty_All T) u = sn ∧ ∀C, lr rhoA (consB C rhoB) T (etapp u). We want lr rhoA rhoB (open_ty T U) (etapp u) = lr rhoA rhoB (open_ty_rec 0 U T) (etapp u). 

The correspondence should be: lr rhoA rhoB (open_ty_rec 0 U T) v ↔ lr rhoA (consB (lr rhoA rhoB U) rhoB) T v. 

Let me re-derive: open_ty_rec 0 U T substitutes U for the free index 0 of T. T's free indices are interpreted in the RHS by (consB C rhoB): index 0 ↦ C = lr U ✓; index (S j) ↦ rhoB j. On the LHS, the opened type has T's index 0 occurrences replaced by U; T's index (S j) occurrences remain at index... hmm, in the opened type, T's free index (S j) stays at index (S j)? But that doesn't make sense: if T is the body of an All, its free indices should be 0 (the binder) and, if T is nested inside another binder... 

Hold on. Let's think about what "free indices" of a type mean here. In lr's recursion, when we're at Ty_All T1, the body T1's indices are relative to the binders *inside* T1 plus the All binder. T1 is a syntactic type; its free indices (relative to its own top level) refer to binders enclosing T1 — but T1 is inside the All, so its free index 0 refers to the All binder, and free index 1 refers to the binder enclosing the All, etc. So when lr interprets T1 with consB C rhoB, it maps index 0 ↦ C and index (S j) ↦ rhoB j ✓ — and rhoB j is the interpretation of the *enclosing* context's index j, which is correct because in the original (unnormalized) type, the enclosing binder was index 1 at T1's level, and in the enclosing context it's index 0 ✓ — so consB shifts ✓.

Now, the correspondence with open_ty_rec: `open_ty_rec k U T` for k=0 replaces the *free* index 0 of T (relative to T's own top level) with U. But when T is a body of an All and lr interprets it with consB, the "free index 0" of T is the All binder, i.e., exactly what consB replaces with C. So the correspondence should be:
   lr rhoA rhoB (open_ty_rec 0 U T) u ↔ lr rhoA (consB (lr rhoA rhoB U) rhoB) T u.
But wait: on the LHS, the type `open_ty_rec 0 U T` — what are its free indices? T's free indices except 0. If T has a free index (S j), then after opening, it remains (S j) in the opened type. But in the RHS's valuation consB C rhoB, T's index (S j) ↦ rhoB j. So for the correspondence to hold, the LHS's free index (S j) would need to be interpreted as rhoB j — but LHS interprets index (S j) as rhoB (S j). So the correspondence FAILS for types with free indices ≥ 1 at the top level.

But can such types arise? In a well-formed closed type, the body of an All has free indices: only 0 at the top level? Let's see: a type like Ty_All T1 where T1 = Ty_All T2: T1's top-level free indices: T1 = Ty_All T2 is a closed-at-top-level constructor (no free index at its top level; the binder in T1 binds inside T2) — so T1's top-level free indices are none! Wait: T1 = Ty_All T2 has no free indices at the top level in the sense of open_ty_rec 0 U T1: open_ty_rec 0 U (Ty_All T2) = Ty_All (open_ty_rec 1 U T2) — so index 0 doesn't occur free at T1's top level (the All binds it... hmm, no: `Ty_All T2` — the All constructor binds index 0 *within T2*. At the top level of `Ty_All T2`, there's no free variable occurrence. So opening with index 0 does nothing at the top level but goes under the binder with k=1.

So the free indices at the top level of a type are determined by its top-level structure: Ty_BVar i is free; Ty_FVar is not an index; constructors bind.

For the body T1 of an All, T1's top-level free indices can be: if T1 = Ty_BVar 0 → index 0 free (the All binder) ✓; if T1 = Ty_BVar (S j) → index S j free — this would mean T1 refers to a binder enclosing the All: possible only if the All itself is inside another binder (i.e., this All is nested inside another All) — and then, in the lr recursion, the *outer* All would have been processed... Let's think: the lr definition processes Ty_All T1 by interpreting T1 with consB C rhoB — where rhoB is the enclosing valuation. T1's free index (S j) is interpreted as rhoB j ✓. So T1 can have top-level free indices ≥ 1, interpreted via the enclosing valuation shifted.

Now, T1's index (S j) refers to an enclosing binder of the All. In the syntactic type `Ty_All T1`, that enclosing binder is... at the level of the whole type `Ty_All T1`, the free indices are those of T1 except the bound ones? Hmm, the constructor Ty_All binds index 0 in T1, so the whole type Ty_All T1's free indices (top-level) = T1's free indices minus 0, decremented by 1? De Bruijn: Ty_All binds index 0 in T1; T1's free index (S j) refers to the binder that, outside the All, is index j. So the whole type's free index j corresponds to T1's index (S j) ✓; and open_ty_rec k U (Ty_All T1) = Ty_All (open_ty_rec (S k) U T1) ✓ consistent.

So in the correspondence lr (open_ty_rec 0 U T1) with the top-level valuation rhoB vs lr T1 with consB C rhoB: T1's index (S j) ↦ rhoB j on the RHS; the opened type's index (S j)... wait, but open_ty_rec 0 U T1 leaves T1's index (S j) as (S j) in the opened type. And the LHS interprets it as rhoB (S j) ≠ rhoB j. ✗ Mismatch!

Hmm, but hold on: what IS the "opened type" here? `open_ty_rec 0 U T1` where T1's top-level free indices can be 0 and S j's. This opened type is a type in the *same* context as the whole `Ty_All T1`?? The free indices of the opened type are T1's free indices except 0: so (S j)'s. But in the context of the whole type `Ty_All T1`, the free indices are T1's minus 0 minus... no. Ugh, the de Bruijn bookkeeping is confusing because open_ty_rec doesn't decrement the indices of the remaining free variables. So `open_ty_rec 0 U T1` is a type whose free indices are "the same numbers" as T1's (except 0 replaced), which is NOT the same as the free indices of the whole type `Ty_All T1`.

The lr definition uses open_ty only in the All case *conceptually* — but I defined lr's All case using the body directly with consB, avoiding open_ty ✓. Chunk 1 content:

```coq
(* ============================================================ *)
(* Erased terms: we strip type annotations. The logical relation *)
(* lives on erased terms, which makes it insensitive to the      *)
(* type annotations and lets us interpret type abstraction by    *)
(* quantification over reducibility candidates.                  *)
(* ============================================================ *)

Inductive etm : Type :=
  | etm_bvar : nat -> etm
  | etm_fvar : atom -> etm
  | etm_abs : etm -> etm
  | etm_app : etm -> etm -> etm
  | etm_tabs : etm -> etm
  | etm_tapp : etm -> etm
  | etm_zero : etm
  | etm_succ : etm -> etm
  | etm_natrec : etm -> etm -> etm -> etm.

Fixpoint erase (t : tm) : etm :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar i => etm_bvar i
  | tm_fvar x => etm_fvar x
  | tm_abs _ t1 => etm_abs (erase t1)
  | tm_app t1 t2 => etm_app (erase t1) (erase t2)
  | tm_tabs t1 => etm_tabs (erase t1)
  | tm_tapp t1 _ => etm_tapp (erase t1)
  | tm_zero => etm_zero
  | tm_succ t1 => etm_succ (erase t1)
  | tm_natrec n b s => etm_natrec (erase n) (erase b) (erase s)
  end.
```

Careful: the match on tm with `_` for the type argument is fine ✓.

Let me draft the complete chunk 1 text:

```coq
(* ============================================================ *)
(* Part 1. Erased syntax and semantics.                          *)
(* ============================================================ *)

Inductive etm : Type :=
  | etm_bvar : nat -> etm
  | etm_fvar : atom -> etm
  | etm_abs : etm -> etm
  | etm_app : etm -> etm -> etm
  | etm_tabs : etm -> etm
  | etm_tapp : etm -> etm
  | etm_zero : etm
  | etm_succ : etm -> etm
  | etm_natrec : etm -> etm -> etm -> etm.

Fixpoint erase (t : tm) : etm :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar i => etm_bvar i
  | tm_fvar x => etm_fvar x
  | tm_abs T t1 => etm_abs (erase t1)
  | tm_app t1 t2 => etm_app (erase t1) (erase t2)
  | tm_tabs t1 => etm_tabs (erase t1)
  | tm_tapp t1 T => etm_tapp (erase t1)
  | tm_zero => etm_zero
  | tm_succ t1 => etm_succ (erase t1)
  | tm_natrec n b s => etm_natrec (erase n) (erase b) (erase s)
  end.

Fixpoint open_e_rec (k : nat) (u t : etm) : etm :=
  match t with
  | etm_bvar i => if Nat.eqb k i then u else etm_bvar i
  | etm_fvar x => etm_fvar x
  | etm_abs t1 => etm_abs (open_e_rec (S k) u t1)
  | etm_app t1 t2 => etm_app (open_e_rec k u t1) (open_e_rec k u t2)
  | etm_tabs t1 => etm_tabs (open_e_rec k u t1)
  | etm_tapp t1 => etm_tapp (open_e_rec k u t1)
  | etm_zero => etm_zero
  | etm_succ t1 => etm_succ (open_e_rec k u t1)
  | etm_natrec n b s => etm_natrec (open_e_rec k u n) (open_e_rec k u b) (open_e_rec k u s)
  end.

Definition open_e (t u : etm) : etm := open_e_rec 0 u t.

Fixpoint subst_e (s : atom -> etm) (t : etm) : etm :=
  match t with
  | etm_bvar i => etm_bvar i
  | etm_fvar x => s x
  | etm_abs t1 => etm_abs (subst_e s t1)
  | etm_app t1 t2 => etm_app (subst_e s t1) (subst_e s t2)
  | etm_tabs t1 => etm_tabs (subst_e s t1)
  | etm_tapp t1 => etm_tapp (subst_e s t1)
  | etm_zero => etm_zero
  | etm_succ t1 => etm_succ (subst_e s t1)
  | etm_natrec n b s0 => etm_natrec (subst_e s n) (subst_e s b) (subst_e s s0)
  end.

Inductive evalue : etm -> Prop :=
  | ev_abs : forall b, evalue (etm_abs b)
  | ev_tabs : forall b, evalue (etm_tabs b)
  | ev_zero : evalue etm_zero
  | ev_succ : forall n, evalue n -> evalue (etm_succ n).

Inductive numeric_value_e : etm -> Prop :=
  | nve_zero : numeric_value_e etm_zero
  | nve_succ : forall n, numeric_value_e n -> numeric_value_e (etm_succ n).

Fixpoint numeral_e (n : nat) : etm :=
  match n with
  | O => etm_zero
  | S m => etm_succ (numeral_e m)
  end.

Inductive estep : etm -> etm -> Prop :=
  | EST_AppAbs : forall b v, evalue v -> estep (etm_app (etm_abs b) v) (open_e b v)
  | EST_App1 : forall t1 t1' t2, estep t1 t1' -> estep (etm_app t1 t2) (etm_app t1' t2)
  | EST_App2 : forall v1 t2 t2', evalue v1 -> estep t2 t2' -> estep (etm_app v1 t2) (etm_app v1 t2')
  | EST_TAppTabs : forall b, estep (etm_tapp (etm_tabs b)) b
  | EST_TApp : forall t t', estep t t' -> estep (etm_tapp t) (etm_tapp t')
  | EST_Succ : forall t t', estep t t' -> estep (etm_succ t) (etm_succ t')
  | EST_RecArg : forall n n' b s, estep n n' -> estep (etm_natrec n b s) (etm_natrec n' b s)
  | EST_RecBase : forall n b b' s, numeric_value_e n -> estep b b' -> estep (etm_natrec n b s) (etm_natrec n b' s)
  | EST_RecStep : forall n b s s', numeric_value_e n -> evalue b -> estep s s' -> estep (etm_natrec n b s) (etm_natrec n b s')
  | EST_RecZero : forall b s, evalue b -> evalue s -> estep (etm_natrec etm_zero b s) b
  | EST_RecSucc : forall n b s, numeric_value_e n -> evalue b -> evalue s ->
      estep (etm_natrec (etm_succ n) b s) (etm_app (etm_app s n) (etm_natrec n b s)).

Inductive sn_e : etm -> Prop :=
  | sn_e_intro : forall u, (forall u', estep u u' -> sn_e u') -> sn_e u.

Hint Constructors evalue numeric_value_e sn_e : core.
```

```coq
Qed.
```

```coq
Definition fresh_atom (l : list atom) : atom := S (fold_right Nat.max 0 l).

Lemma fresh_atom_notin : forall l, ~ In (fresh_atom l) l.
Proof.
  intros l H. assert (Hle : forall x, In x l -> x <= fold_right Nat.max 0 l).
  { clear H. induction l as [| a l IH]; intros x Hx.
    - contradiction.
    - simpl in Hx. destruct Hx as [-> | Hx].
      + apply Nat.le_max_l.
      + eapply Nat.le_trans; [apply IH; eassumption | apply Nat.le_max_r]. }
  specialize (Hle _ H). unfold fresh_atom in H. lia.
Qed.
```

```coq
Lemma lc_ty_open_pres : forall T k U,
  lc_ty_at 0 U -> lc_ty_at (S k) T -> lc_ty_at k (open_ty_rec k U T).
Proof.

    + apply Nat.eqb_eq in E. subst. apply lc_ty_at_mono with (k := 0); [lia | assumption].
    + apply lc_ty_bvar. inversion HT; subst. apply Nat.eqb_neq in E. lia.
  - (* fvar *) constructor.
  - (* arrow *) inversion HT; subst. constructor; auto.
  - (* all *) inversion HT; subst. constructor. apply IHT with (k := S k); auto.
  - (* nat *) constructor.
Qed.
```

4. lc_ty_open_refl:

```coq
Lemma lc_ty_open_refl : forall T k U,
  lc_ty_at k (open_ty_rec k U T) -> lc_ty_at (S k) T.

  - inversion H; subst. constructor; auto.

  - constructor.
Qed.
```

Let me define the patterns carefully:
has_type constructors:
- T_Var : forall Delta Gamma x T, lookup_context x Gamma = Some T -> wf_ty Delta T -> has_type Delta Gamma (tm_fvar x) T (no recursive args)
- T_Abs : forall (L) Delta Gamma T1 t2 T2, wf_ty Delta T1 -> (forall x, ~In x L -> has_type Delta (update Gamma x T1) (open_tm t2 (fvar x)) T2) -> has_type ... (one recursive arg — but it's inside a function type, so it's "non-uniform": the IH is `forall x, ~In x L -> P(x)`).
- T_App : t1 t2 T1 T2, has_type ... t1 (Arrow T1 T2) -> has_type ... t2 T1 -> has_type ... (App t1 t2) T2: two recursive args → pattern: H1 IH1 H2 IH2.
- T_TAbs : L Delta Gamma t T, (forall X, ~In X L -> has_type (X::Delta) Gamma (open_tm_ty t (fvar X)) (open_ty T (fvar X))) -> ...: one recursive under a forall.
- T_TApp : t T U, has_type ... t (All T) -> wf_ty Delta U -> ...: one recursive arg then the wf: pattern H1 IH1 Hwf.
- T_Zero: none.
- T_Succ: n, has_type ... n Nat -> ...: pattern Hn IHn.
- T_Rec: n b s T, has_type n Nat -> has_type b T -> has_type s (...) -> ...: three recursive args: pattern Hn IHn Hb IHb Hs IHs (interleaved: H1 IH1 H2 IH2 H3 IH3) ✓ that's what I wrote.

The induction scheme: forall k T1 T2, P k T1 -> P k T2 -> ... Since the constructor is `lc_ty_arrow : forall k T1 T2, lc_ty_at k T1 -> lc_ty_at k T2 -> lc_ty_at k (Ty_Arrow T1 T2)`, the arguments are k, T1, T2 then the recursive premises. Let me think about the `lr` Fixpoint definition in Coq syntax:

```coq
Fixpoint lr (rhoA : atom -> etm -> Prop) (rhoB : nat -> etm -> Prop) (T : ty) : etm -> Prop :=
  match T with
  | Ty_BVar i => rhoB i
  | Ty_FVar X => rhoA X
  | Ty_Arrow T1 T2 =>
      fun u => sn_e u /\ (forall v, lr rhoA rhoB T1 v -> lr rhoA rhoB T2 (etm_app u v))
  | Ty_All T1 =>
      fun u => sn_e u /\ (forall C, cand C -> lr rhoA (consB C rhoB) T1 (etm_tapp u))
  | Ty_Nat => sn_e
  end.
```

Coq might complain about the recursive calls not being on syntactically smaller arguments — they are (T1, T2 are subterms of the match scrutinee T) ✓.

Then:
```coq
Definition cand_val (rhoA : atom -> etm -> Prop) (rhoB : nat -> etm -> Prop) : Prop :=
  (forall X, cand (rhoA X)) /\ (forall i, cand (rhoB i)).
```

```coq
Lemma lr_mapB_equiv : forall T rhoA rho rho',
  (forall i u, rho i u <-> rho' i u) ->
  forall u, lr rhoA rho T u <-> lr rhoA rho' T u.
Proof.
  induction T as [n | X | T1 IH1 T2 IH2 | T1 IH1 | ]; intros rhoA rho rho' Hagree u; simpl.
  - apply Hagree.
  - reflexivity.
  - split; intros [Hsn Hcl]; split; [assumption | | assumption |].
    + intros v Hv. apply (proj1 (IH2 rhoA rho rho' Hagree (etm_app u v))). apply Hcl.
      apply (proj2 (IH1 rhoA rho rho' Hagree v)). assumption.
    + intros v Hv. apply (proj2 (IH2 ...)). apply Hcl. apply (proj1 (IH1 ...)). assumption.
  - split; intros [Hsn Hcl]; split; [assumption | | assumption |].
    + intros C HC. apply (proj1 (IH1 rhoA (consB C rho) (consB C rho') ?Hagree (etm_tapp u))).
      apply Hcl. assumption.
    + ...
  - reflexivity.
Qed.
```

Let me structure as a single lemma with all hypotheses explicit, doing nested inductions:

Lemma lr_natrec_numeral : forall k b, sn_e b ->
  forall s, sn_e s ->
  forall rhoA rhoB T, cand_val rhoA rhoB ->
    lr rhoA rhoB T b ->
    lr rhoA rhoB (Ty_Arrow Ty_Nat (Ty_Arrow T T)) s ->
    lr rhoA rhoB T (etm_natrec (numeral_e k) b s).
Proof.
  induction k as [| k' IHk].
  - intros b Hsnb. induction Hsnb as [b Hstepb IHb].
    intros s Hsns. induction Hsns as [s Hsteps IHs].
    intros rhoA rhoB T Hcv Hb Hs.
    ...
  - intros b Hsnb. induction Hsnb as [b Hstepb IHb].
    intros s Hsns. induction Hsns as [s Hsteps IHs].
    intros rhoA rhoB T Hcv Hb Hs.
    ...
Qed.

Definition env_rel (rhoA rhoB) (sb : atom -> etm) (Gamma : context) : Prop :=
  forall x T, lookup_context x Gamma = Some T -> lr rhoA rhoB T (sb x).

Theorem fundamental : forall Delta Gamma t T, has_type Delta Gamma t T ->
  forall rhoA rhoB sb,
    cand_val rhoA rhoB ->
    env_rel rhoA rhoB sb Gamma ->
    lr rhoA rhoB T (subst_e sb (erase t)).

Proof by induction on the typing derivation.

Cases:
- T_Var: apply (Henv x T Hlk).
- T_Abs: need lr (T1→T2) (eabs (subst_e sb (erase body))).
  split: sn_e (eabs ...) ✓ ev_abs. intros v Hv.
  Choose x := fresh_atom (L ++ fv_e (erase body)).
  Derive ~In x L and ~In x (fv_e (erase body)).
  Define sb' := fun y => if Nat.eqb y x then v else sb y.
  Have IHbody := IHb x HxL sb' Hcv (env_rel extension).
  IHbody : lr rhoA rhoB T2 (subst_e sb' (erase (open_tm body (tm_fvar x)))).
  Rewrite: erase (open_tm body (fvar x)) = open_e (erase body) (efvar x).
  So IHbody : lr T2 (subst_e sb' (open_e (erase body) (efvar x))).
  Rewrite using subst_open_e: subst_e sb' (open_e b (efvar x)) = open_e (subst_e sb b) v where b = erase body ✓.
  Then use lr_app_abs with Hbody := fun v Hv => that ✓ — but we need the body hypothesis as a function of v: for each v, we applied the IH at sb'_v. Since the IH is universally quantified over sb', we can do it per v ✓.
  
  In Coq, we need to prove `forall v, lr T1 v -> lr T2 (open_e (subst_e sb (erase body)) v)`: intro v; intro Hv; instantiate the IH.
  Then apply (lr_app_abs T1 T2 rhoA rhoB (subst_e sb (erase body)) Hcv Hbody v Hv) ✓.

- T_App: IH1 gives lr (T1→T2) (...); apply its second component to IH2 ✓.
- T_TAbs: as described.
- T_TApp: as described.
- T_Zero: lr Nat etm_zero = sn_e etm_zero ✓ (sn_e_evalue ev_zero). Note subst_e sb (erase tm_zero) = etm_zero ✓ (simpl).
- T_Succ: IH : sn_e (subst_e sb (erase n)); need sn_e (etm_succ (subst_e sb (erase n))) via sn_e_succ ✓.
- T_Rec: natrec lemma.

Hmm, this is the classic issue: the opened term's free bvars could be captured by the substitution. Let me re-examine the lemma: 
LHS: subst_e sb' (open_e_rec k (efvar x) b) — opening b at depth k with the variable x, then substituting sb'.
RHS: open_e_rec k v (subst_e sb b) — substituting sb into b, then opening with v.

In the fvar case, b = etm_fvar y: LHS = subst_e sb' (etm_fvar y) = sb' y; RHS = open_e_rec k v (sb y). These are equal only if `open_e_rec k v (sb y) = sb y` — which holds if sb y has no free bvar k... not guaranteed!

Hmm. So my lemma is wrong in general. The issue: opening a term at depth k with a term that may contain free variables... wait, in the LHS, the opened term open_e_rec k (efvar x) b doesn't contain v at all; the substitution sb' replaces free variables including x. Since x ∉ FV(b), the only x in the opened term is the substituted one (from the opening). So LHS = subst_e sb b' where the opened bvar is replaced by... hold on: opening replaces bvar k occurrences with efvar x; then substitution sb' replaces all free variables by sb'; the occurrence of x becomes v. Meanwhile all other free variables y of b become sb' y = sb y (for y ≠ x) — where sb y can be an arbitrary term. Then the RHS opens sb y at depth k with v — but sb y's free variables could include bvar k (a dangling variable)! Hmm, but sb y is supposed to be a *closed-ish* substitution image... In our application, sb is the environment (any atom → etm), so sb y could indeed contain free bvars — but those bvars would be dangling in an ill-scoped way. Since we're working with erased terms without lc conditions, this could actually break.

Hmm. Let me reconsider the opening/substitution commutation in the presence of de Bruijn indices. The standard lemma requires the substituted terms to be "locally closed" (no free bvars) or uses proper lifting/shifting. In our setting, the terms in the relation may have free bvars (e.g., lr doesn't require lc). Hmm.

Wait, does it matter? Let's look at where this lemma is used: the T_Abs case. We have the IH at sb' = [x↦v]sb giving lr T2 (subst_e sb' (erase (open_tm body (fvar x)))).   | lc_e_zero ...
  | lc_e_succ ...
  | lc_e_natrec ...
Definition lc_e t := lc_e_at 0 t.

Definition cand (P : etm -> Prop) : Prop :=
  (forall u, P u -> lc_e u) /\
  (forall u, P u -> sn_e u) /\
  (forall u u', P u -> estep u u' -> P u') /\
  (forall u, lc_e u -> ~ evalue u -> (forall u', estep u u' -> P u') -> P u).

And lr:
  | Ty_BVar i => fun u => lc_e u /\ rhoB i u
  | Ty_FVar X => fun u => lc_e u /\ rhoA X u
  | Ty_Nat => fun u => lc_e u /\ sn_e u
  | Ty_Arrow A B => fun u => lc_e u /\ sn_e u /\ (forall v, lr A v -> lr B (eapp u v))
  | Ty_All A => fun u => lc_e u /\ sn_e u /\ (forall C, cand C -> lr (consB C rhoB) A (etapp u))

Note: with lr A v including lc_e v, the arrow case automatically restricts to lc v ✓.

cand_val := (∀X, cand (rhoA X)) ∧ (∀i, cand (rhoB i)).

Let me carefully design the new definitions.

```coq
(* Part 2B: locality for erased terms, and well-typed terms are locally closed *)

Inductive lc_e_at : nat -> etm -> Prop :=
  | lc_e_bvar : forall k i, i < k -> lc_e_at k (etm_bvar i)
  | lc_e_fvar : forall k x, lc_e_at k (etm_fvar x)
  | lc_e_abs : forall k b, lc_e_at (S k) b -> lc_e_at k (etm_abs b)
  | lc_e_app : forall k b1 b2, lc_e_at k b1 -> lc_e_at k b2 -> lc_e_at k (etm_app b1 b2)
  | lc_e_tabs : forall k b, lc_e_at k b -> lc_e_at k (etm_tabs b)
  | lc_e_tapp : forall k b, lc_e_at k b -> lc_e_at k (etm_tapp b)
  | lc_e_zero : forall k, lc_e_at k etm_zero
  | lc_e_succ : forall k b, lc_e_at k b -> lc_e_at k (etm_succ b)
  | lc_e_natrec : forall k b1 b2 b3, lc_e_at k b1 -> lc_e_at k b2 -> lc_e_at k b3 -> lc_e_at k (etm_natrec b1 b2 b3).

Definition lc_e (t : etm) := lc_e_at 0 t.

Hint Constructors lc_e_at : core.
```

Lemmas:
1. lc_e_at_mono : forall k t, lc_e_at k t -> forall k', k <= k' -> lc_e_at k' t.
2. lc_e_at_open : forall b k u, lc_e_at (S k) b -> lc_e u -> lc_e_at k (open_e_rec k u b).
   Hmm, careful: the lc_e of u is at 0; under deeper openings (k increases), u must be lc at that depth ✓ by mono.
   Proof by induction on b, generalizing k:
   - bvar i: lc_e_at (S k) (bvar i) → i < S k. open: if k =? i then u else bvar i. If i = k: u, need lc_e_at k u ✓ (mono from 0). Else i < k ✓.
   - fvar ✓; abs: IH at S k ✓; app: IH; tabs: IH at same k ✓ (tabs doesn't bind term vars); tapp ✓; zero ✓; succ ✓; natrec ✓.
3. lc_e_subst : forall b k sb, lc_e_at k b -> (forall x, lc_e (sb x)) -> lc_e_at k (subst_e sb b).
   - bvar ✓; fvar: sb x is lc at 0 → mono k ✓; abs: IH at S k ✓; etc.
4. lc_e_open_id : forall t, lc_e t -> forall k u, open_e_rec k u t = t.
   - bvar i: lc_e_at 0 (bvar i) → i < 0 → absurd. Hmm, the statement with k: open_e_rec k u (bvar i) = if k =? i then u else bvar i. With lc_e t = lc_e_at 0 t, for a bvar, impossible. But under binders we'd need lc_e_at (S k) b... Let me generalize: forall t k, lc_e_at k t -> forall j u, j >= k? Hmm. The needed use: lc_e (sb y) → open_e_rec k v (sb y) = sb y for any k. So I need: lc_e t → forall k u, open_e_rec k u t = t. For t = bvar i with lc_e → i < 0 impossible ✓ (so vacuous). For t = abs b: lc_e (abs b) → lc_e_at 1 b; open_e_rec k u (abs b) = abs (open_e_rec (S k) u b); need open_e_rec (S k) u b = b — from lc_e_at 1 b, which is stronger than lc_e (0)... Hmm, my IH is for lc_e (at 0). So I need the generalized statement: forall t k, lc_e_at k t -> forall j, k <= j -> forall u, open_e_rec j u t = t.
   Proof: bvar i: i < k ≤ j → i ≠ j ✓. abs: lc_e_at k (abs b) → lc_e_at (S k) b; j ≥ k → S j ≥ S k; IH ✓. tabs: lc_e_at k b; j ≥ k; IH ✓. etc. ✓
5. lc_e_at_step : forall u, lc_e u -> forall u', estep u u' -> lc_e u'. 
   Proof by induction on the step derivation? Since the step is an inductive, and we'd need lc of subterms. Let's do by induction on estep:
   - EST_AppAbs: lc_e (eapp (eabs b) v) → lc_e (eabs b) and lc_e v → lc_e_at 1 b and lc_e v → lc_e (open_e b v) via lc_e_at_open ✓.
   - EST_App1: lc (app t1 t2) → lc t1, lc t2; IH (from the induction on the step) gives lc t1'; then lc (app t1' t2) ✓.
   - EST_App2: lc v1, lc t2; IH ✓.
   - EST_TAppTabs: lc (tapp (tabs b)) → lc (tabs b) → lc b ✓.
   - EST_TApp: IH ✓.

Design details for the new lr:

Definition lr (rhoA) (rhoB) (T) : etm -> Prop :=
  match T with
  | Ty_BVar i => fun u => lc_e u /\ rhoB i u
  | Ty_FVar X => fun u => lc_e u /\ rhoA X u
  | Ty_Arrow T1 T2 => fun u => lc_e u /\ sn_e u /\ (forall v, lr rhoA rhoB T1 v -> lr rhoA rhoB T2 (etm_app u v))
  | Ty_All T1 => fun u => lc_e u /\ sn_e u /\ (forall C, cand C -> lr rhoA (consB C rhoB) T1 (etm_tapp u))
  | Ty_Nat => fun u => lc_e u /\ sn_e u
  end.

cand:
  (forall u, P u -> lc_e u) /\
  (forall u, P u -> sn_e u) /\
  (forall u u', P u -> estep u u' -> P u') /\
  (forall u, lc_e u -> ~ evalue u -> (forall u', estep u u' -> P u') -> P u).

Accessors: cand_lc, cand_sn, cand_step, cand_expand.

cand_sn_e: cand (fun u => lc_e u /\ sn_e u) ✓.

cand_lr:
- BVar: cand (fun u => lc_e u ∧ ρ i u): from cand (ρ i):
  split; [intros u [Hlc Hp] ; assumption |]; split; [intros u [Hlc Hp]; apply Hsn |]; split; [intros u u' [Hlc Hp] Hst; split; [apply lc_e_step| apply (step)] |]; intros u Hlc Hne Hall; split; [assumption | apply (expand) with Hlc; intros u' Hst; apply (proj2 (Hall u' Hst))].
- FVar similar.
- Nat: as above.
- Arrow: P u = lc u ∧ sn u ∧ ∀v, ...
  CR-lc: proj1.
  CR-sn: proj1 of proj2.
  CR2: given (Hlc, Hsn, Hcl) and estep u u':
    split; [lc_e_step ✓|]; split; [sn_e_step ✓|]; intros v Hv; apply (cand_step (lr T2) ...) with (u := eapp u v): need lr T2 (eapp u v) [Hcl v Hv] and estep (eapp u v) (eapp u' v) ✓.
  CR3': given Hlc : lc_e u, Hne, Hall.
    split; [assumption |]; split; [apply sn_e_intro; intros u' Hst; exact (cand_sn (lr T) (cand_lr T) u' (Hall u' Hst)) |];
    intros v Hv.
    apply (cand_expand (lr T2) (cand_lr T2) (eapp u v)).
    - apply lc_e_at_app? We need lc_e (eapp u v) — from Hlc and cand_lc (lr T1) (cand_lr T1) v Hv ✓. (lc_e is preserved... just constructors.)
    - apply not_evalue_app.
    - intros w Hw; destruct (estep_app_inv u v w Hw) as [...]:
      * AppAbs: u = abs b → evalue u contradict Hne. Hmm, we get u = etm_abs b; then evalue u via ev_abs ✓ → contradiction.
      * App1: estep u u' → Hall u' Hst gives the arrow relation; apply its third component to v Hv ✓.
      * App2: evalue u → contradiction ✓.
- All: P u = lc u ∧ sn u ∧ ∀C cand, lr (consB C) A (tapp u).
  CR3': given Hlc, Hne, Hall; for C HC:
    apply (cand_expand (lr rhoA (consB C rhoB) A) (cand_lr A ...)):
    - lc_e (tapp u) ✓.
    - not_evalue_tapp.
    - intros w Hw; destruct (estep_tapp_inv u w Hw): tabs → evalue u contrad; tapp → Hall u' Hst's third component ✓.

lr_sn: lr T u → sn_e u: from cand_sn ✓ (cand_lr). Since lr's each case has sn as a conjunct ✓ but cand_sn is uniform ✓.
lr_lc: lr T u → lc_e u ✓ similarly.
lr_step ✓.

```
Lemma lr_mapB_equiv : forall T rhoA rho rho',
  (forall i u, rho i u <-> rho' i u) ->
  forall u, lr rhoA rho T u <-> lr rhoA rho' T u.
Proof.
  induction T as [n | X | T1 IH1 T2 IH2 | T1 IH1 | ];
    intros rhoA rho rho' Hagree u; simpl.
  - split; intros [HA HB]; split; [assumption | | assumption |].
    + apply (Hagree n u) in HB. assumption.
    + apply (Hagree n u). assumption.
  - reflexivity.
  - split; intros [HA [HB HC]]; split; [assumption | split | assumption | split].
    + assumption.
    + intros v Hv. apply (proj1 (IH2 rhoA rho rho' Hagree (etm_app u v))).
      apply HC. apply (proj2 (IH1 rhoA rho rho' Hagree v)). assumption.
    + assumption.
    + intros v Hv. apply (proj2 (IH2 rhoA rho rho' Hagree (etm_app u v))).
      apply HC. apply (proj1 (IH1 rhoA rho rho' Hagree v)). assumption.
  - split; intros [HA [HB HC]]; split; [assumption | split | assumption | split].
    + assumption.
    + intros C HCc. apply (proj1 (IH1 rhoA (consB C rho) (consB C rho') (consB_equiv C rho rho' Hagree) (etm_tapp u))).
      apply HC. assumption.
    + assumption.
    + intros C HCc. apply (proj2 (IH1 rhoA (consB C rho) (consB C rho') (consB_equiv C rho rho' Hagree) (etm_tapp u))).
      apply HC. assumption.
  - reflexivity.
Qed.
```
Note `split; [assumption | split | assumption | split]` — the syntax `split; [t1 | t2 | t3 | t4]` applies t1 to the first goal, t2 to the second, etc. With 2 goals from the outer split, we need only 2 tactics...   - split.

```

    + intros [HA HB]. split.
      * assumption.

```
Wait: goal 2 is rho' n u, HB : rho n u → apply (Hagree n u) gives goal `rho n u`? Applying an iff A <-> B to a goal B: `apply H` where H : A <-> B and the goal is B — Coq uses the *forward* direction? `apply` with an iff: if the goal matches the RHS, it leaves the LHS. So goal rho' n u with Hagree : rho n u <-> rho' n u → apply leaves rho n u ✓ then assumption ✓.
For the reverse: goal rho n u, apply (Hagree n u) — the goal matches the LHS; Coq's apply uses the backward direction only if the goal matches the RHS. For the LHS match, `apply` would need B → A, which is not directly available; but `apply` with an iff can use the *converse*? Actually `apply H` where H : A <-> B: Coq will try to unify the goal with B (then leave A) or with A (then leave B) — I believe it tries both directions ✓. Actually, in Coq, `apply` on an iff works both ways since <-> is a conjunction of two implications and apply can use either ✓ (it normalizes iff to and of implications and searches). Let me write the FT carefully.

```coq
Definition env_rel (rhoA : atom -> etm -> Prop) (rhoB : nat -> etm -> Prop)
  (sb : atom -> etm) (Gamma : context) : Prop :=
  (forall y, lc_e (sb y)) /\
  (forall x T, lookup_context x Gamma = Some T -> lr rhoA rhoB T (sb x)).

Lemma env_rel_update : forall rhoA rhoB sb Gamma x T1 v,
  env_rel rhoA rhoB sb Gamma ->
  lr rhoA rhoB T1 v ->
  env_rel rhoA rhoB (fun y => if Nat.eqb y x then v else sb y) (update Gamma x T1).
Proof.
  intros rhoA rhoB sb Gamma x T1 v [Hlc Henv] Hv. split.
  - intros y. destruct (Nat.eqb y x) eqn:E.
    + apply lr_lc with (rhoA := rhoA) (rhoB := rhoB) (T := T1). assumption.
    + apply Hlc.
  - intros y A Hlk. simpl in Hlk.
    destruct (Nat.eqb y x) eqn:E.
    + apply Nat.eqb_eq in E. subst y. injection Hlk as HA. subst A.
      rewrite Nat.eqb_refl. simpl. assumption.
    + apply Nat.eqb_neq in E.
      apply Henv with (x := y). assumption.
Qed.
```

Hmm, the goal in the second part: `lr rhoA rhoB A ((fun y => if Nat.eqb y x then v else sb y) y)` — after `destruct (Nat.eqb y x)`, the goal reduces to either `lr ... T1 v` (if true) or `lr ... A (sb y)` (if false) ✓. But the `(fun y => ...) y` beta-reduces ✓; then the if with the destructed equation ✓.

Note: `lookup_context y (update Gamma x T1)` — update is a Definition; `simpl in Hlk` unfolds update and lookup_context ✓ giving `(if y =? x then Some T1 else lookup_context y Gamma) = Some A` ✓. Then `destruct (Nat.eqb y x)` — hmm, the term in Hlk after simpl is `Nat.eqb y x`? lookup_context uses `Nat.eqb x y` (looking at the definition: `if Nat.eqb x y then Some T else lookup_context x Gamma'` with x the lookup variable and y the binder). Hmm: lookup_context x ((y,T)::Γ) = if Nat.eqb x y then Some T else lookup_context x Γ'. With update Γ x T1 = (x,T1)::Γ, lookup_context y ((x,T1)::Γ) = if Nat.eqb y x then Some T1 else lookup_context y Γ ✓. So the eqb is `Nat.eqb y x` ✓ matching my destruct.

Then for the false branch: `apply Nat.eqb_neq in E` gives y ≠ x; and Hlk : lookup_context y Γ = Some A ✓; then Henv y A Hlk ✓.

For the true branch: E : Nat.eqb y x = true → y = x; subst; Hlk : Some T1 = Some A → A = T1 (injection) ✓. The goal: lr rhoA rhoB A (if Nat.eqb x x then v else sb x) — with A = T1 and Nat.eqb x x reducing... `rewrite Nat.eqb_refl; simpl` — hmm, after subst, the term is `(fun y0 => if Nat.eqb y0 x then v else sb y0) x` = `if Nat.eqb x x then v else sb x`; but the destruct on `Nat.eqb y x` with y = x... Since the destruct happened before subst, the goal's if would be `if true then v else sb x`? Hmm, the destruct on `Nat.eqb y x` in the goal: Coq generalizes the goal over this term, so the goal has the match on the destructed boolean; after destruct with y replaced... it's complicated.

Simplest: avoid the `destruct` on the goal and instead rewrite in the goal:
```
  - intros y A Hlk. simpl in Hlk.
    destruct (Nat.eqb y x) eqn:E.
    + apply Nat.eqb_eq in E. subst y. injection Hlk as HA. subst A.
      simpl. rewrite Nat.eqb_refl. simpl. assumption.
    + apply Nat.eqb_neq in E.
      apply Henv with (x := y). assumption.
```
Let me test this and adapt.

```coq
Theorem fundamental : forall Delta Gamma t T, has_type Delta Gamma t T ->
  forall rhoA rhoB sb,
```

```coq
Fixpoint fv_context (Gamma : context) : list atom :=
  match Gamma with
  | [] => []
  | (x, T) :: Gamma' => fv_ty T ++ fv_context Gamma'
  end.

Lemma env_rel_updA : forall rhoA rhoB sb Gamma X C,
  ~ In X (fv_context Gamma) ->
  env_rel rhoA rhoB sb Gamma ->
  env_rel (updA rhoA X C) rhoB sb Gamma.
Proof.
  intros rhoA rhoB sb Gamma X C Hfresh [Hlc Henv]. split; [assumption |].
  intros x T Hlk.
  apply (proj1 (lr_updA_irrel T X C rhoA rhoB _ (sb x))).
  - intro HH. apply Hfresh. 
    (* need In X (fv_context Gamma) from lookup x Γ = Some T and In X (fv_ty T) *)
    induction Gamma as [| [y Ty] Gamma' IH]; simpl in *.
    + simpl in Hlk. discriminate.
    + simpl in Hlk. destruct (Nat.eqb x y) eqn:E.
      * injection Hlk as HT. subst T. simpl. apply in_app_l. assumption.
      * apply in_app_r. apply IH. assumption.
  - apply Henv. assumption.
Qed.
```

Lemma in_fv_context : forall Gamma x T X,
  lookup_context x Gamma = Some T -> In X (fv_ty T) -> In X (fv_context Gamma).
Proof.
  induction Gamma as [| [y Ty] Gamma' IH]; intros x T X Hlk HX; simpl in *.
  - discriminate.
  - destruct (Nat.eqb x y) eqn:E.
    + injection Hlk as HT. subst T. apply in_app_l. assumption.
    + apply in_app_r. apply IH with (x := x); assumption.
Qed.

Then env_rel_updA.

via in_app_l/in_app_r (with the right associativity: L ++ fv_ty T ++ fv_context Gamma = L ++ (fv_ty T ++ fv_context Gamma)).

```coq
Lemma sn_erase : forall u, sn_e u -> forall t, erase t = u -> strongly_normalizing t.
Proof.
  intros u H. induction H as [u Hstep IH]. intros t Heq.
  apply SN_intro. intros t' Ht'.
  apply IH with (u' := erase t').
  - rewrite <- Heq. apply erase_step. assumption.
  - reflexivity.
Qed.
```

Let me summarize the approach for the user:

1. **Type erasure**: Defined an erased term syntax `etm` and erased CBV semantics `estep`, with a projection lemma `step t t' → estep (erase t) (erase t')` and transfer of strong normalization back (`sn_e (erase t) → strongly_normalizing t`).

2. **Locality**: Defined local closedness `lc_e` for erased terms; proved it's preserved by step. The logical relation includes `lc_e` (necessary because the language's opening operation doesn't shift de Bruijn indices, so capture-avoidance requires locally closed substitutes).

3. **Logical relation**: A unary type-indexed relation `lr ρA ρB T` where free type variables are interpreted by a candidate valuation `ρA : atom → etm → Prop` and bound type variables by `ρB : nat → etm → Prop`. The universal type is interpreted by quantification over all reducibility candidates, with the bound variable valuation extended (`consB`); the recursive body is used directly (no syntactic type substitution needed).

4. **Reducibility candidates**: `cand P` = locally closed + SN + closed under reduction + closed under head expansion (for non-values).

5. **Key lemmas**:
   - `cand_lr`: every `lr ρ T` is a candidate (structural induction on T).
   - Valuation irrelevance and "opening correspondence" `lr ρ (open_ty T U) u ↔ lr (ρ with 0 ↦ lr U) T u`.
   - `lr_natrec_numeral` / `lr_natrec`: closure under primitive recursion, by induction on the numeral and nested induction on the SN derivations of the base/step terms.
   - `lr_app_abs`: beta-expansion for applications of abstractions.
   - Substitution/opening commutation.

6. **Fundamental theorem**: for every typing derivation, in every candidate-valued type valuation and every term-substitution environment satisfying the context, the substituted erased term is in the relation.

7. **Final theorem**: instantiate with the empty context, identity substitution, and the SN candidate; conclude `strongly_normalizing t`.
